I just found out you can use your Steam Wishlist to search for deals on games outside of Steam, saving my nephew more than £40 on Total War: Warhammer 3

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It earned me a bucketload of uncle points

I may be very late to the party with this discovery, but I only just realised you can connect your Steam Wishlist to a third-party deals tracker. The beauty of which is that, once hooked up, you can see what games you've been pining for are going cheap not just on Steam but through any major shopfront across the internet.

I worry that I will be coming across as 'Use this one trick to save money – greengrocers hate it!', but it's already helped save my nephew more than £40. Even if it meant using a slightly underhand method.

Two weeks ago, I got a text from my nephew asking what the best way to play Total War: Warhammer 3. When I messaged back a couple of hours later (look, I'm not a responsive uncle) to tell him it's currently £50 on Steam, but it's worth waiting because according to SteamDB's price history it's come down to £7.50 in the past, I was too late. He'd got itchy feet and bought it at full whack on Steam, using up all his birthday money.

That's when I went looking and found Is There Any Deal for the first time. (A lot of you in the comments may be throwing your hands up in despair to hear this oversight) If you've not come across it before, it's a site that tracks game sales across loads of shops, including Steam, EA Store, Epic Games Store, GamersGate, GOG. GreenManGaming, Humble. This means while a game may not be on sale on Steam you can see if it's on sale anywhere else.

In the case of Total War: Warhammer 3, while it was full price on Steam, over on the Humble Store it was discounted to £7.49. I sent a link to my nephew and then told him, using the authority only an uncle has, that it's okay to refund the game through Steam and buy the cheaper version. It's not something I've ever done myself, but he's 17 and that is a lot of money to save.

Anyway, that's recommendation the first. It was only when I was looking for a way to export my Steam Wishlist to ask RPS supporters what I should cull that I realised Is There Any Deal has another feature: you can import every game on your Wishlist to get deal notifications. (This may get a second groan of despair in the comment section.) Once linked up, the site will ping you a notification whenever games you're waiting for pop up in a deal.

Just looking at my current list, I can get Genesis Noir, Ancient Enemy, and Norco for £10 total, all of which with more than 70% discounts through stores other than Steam. So, as you can see, this could be both a saving technically and lead me to spending a lot more money than I mean to…

You may already have heard of these tools, but as I hadn't I thought it best to give them a shout out.

Now considering I've revealed myself to be a rube, what other third party tools should I be installing to weaponise my Steam Library?

How a new Pulte Florida agrihood makes conservation a buyer desire

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

PulteGroup is developing The Grow, a 1,200-acre agrihood east of Orlando planned for about 2,200 homes anchored by a 9-acre working farm. Plans leave just over half the site undeveloped, positioning the project as a growth and conservation case study.  AI Summary

For years, Central Florida’s exploding population growth and rapidly expanding suburban sprawl have clashed with local efforts to protect the region’s dwindling green space and undeveloped land. PulteGroup’s new 1,200-acre community on Orlando’s eastern edge aims to balance these seemingly contradictory interests.

The community is called The Grow. Once undeveloped, rural land, the property is set to begin morphing into a roughly 2,200-home “agrihood” centered around a 9-acre working farm. In keeping with its conservation focus, the plan calls for reserving just over half of the site from development, including wetlands, conservation areas and shared green space.

The Grow offers a potential case study in balancing growth and conservation, a tension deeply felt in Central Florida and many other high-growth Sun Belt markets. Between 2000 and 2025, the Orlando metro area’s population grew nearly 80%, from 1.64 million to 2.95 million.

That growth reshaped once-rural communities on the region’s edges, replacing undeveloped land with new subdivisions, apartments and shopping centers. Many long-time Sunshine State residents now lament the loss of “Old Florida,” marked by a landscape of open land, working ranches and orange groves that are now developed.

When Pulte, the sole homebuilder at The Grow, purchased the community’s lots in 2023, it set out to build a premier community that responded to these tensions.

“One of our biggest selling features is the openness of the community and how much greenery we left. Everywhere you look, you’re surrounded by trees,” Patrick McNamara, a Sales Manager at Pulte, told HousingWire TBD.

However, the true distinguisher between The Grow and other local communities will be its working farm, a rare amenity that carries the conservation theme beyond protecting land to putting it to use.

For Pulte, which held a grand opening for the community in late September, the farm and the extensive conservation space give the builder a way to stand out to prospective homebuyers, a crucial edge considering the challenging sales environment homebuilders are facing.

The working farm is central to the community’s agrihood concept – providing fresh, sustainably grown produce. Residents will support it by buying products, volunteering and joining educational activities.

McNamara argued that the farm creates a direct connection between residents and their food. A professional third-party company will manage the farm, but residents and families will be allowed to volunteer and then buy the finished produce at a resident-only general store that will serve as an anchor for The Grow’s commercial and retail street.

The development will include two other farms: a small demonstration farm, covering about an eighth of an acre, and a separate community farm where homeowners can practice what they learn.

These smaller farms will serve as an educational, hands-on amenity for families, where residents can learn each stage of growing produce and then put what they learn into practice. An existing program, “Seed the Table,” will allow residents to plant crops, watch them grow and eat what they produced.

The farmers will also work with the lifestyle director to hold chef-led cooking classes at the community’s gathering barn, and will host seasonal celebrations and festivals that bring homeowners together.

Beyond conservation and sustainability, the farm is meant to build community, encourage learning and offer a unique lifestyle.

The Grow, which features homes from the low $400s to the high $800s, isn’t immune to the macro-level sales pressures impacting the local and national housing market. McNamara concedes that his team has to leverage generous incentives like mortgage rate buydowns to finish sales.

However, he also confirmed that The Grow has sold more than 100 homes since opening pre-sales about six months ago, and that prospective buyers have been anticipating the community’s opening for years.

The agrihood concept is the hook that draws buyers in.

“The farm is a big draw,” McNamara said. “I think the agrihood is what’s bringing people in, but what’s getting them to buy is once they see everything else that comes with it.”

In addition to the working farm and conservation space, The Grow will feature a main street with about 170,000 square feet of retail and commercial space, envisioned as a high-end retail and dining corridor that can work as a visitor magnet.

Other features include an indoor event and social space, an outdoor green space with a community pizza oven, a dedicated dog park, walking and nature trails, playgrounds and other resident-only amenities like a clubhouse, a gym and a pool.

“[Buyers] are shocked to find out that this isn’t a little farm community. This is a high-end resort neighborhood.”

The community’s preservation of nature is another big selling point. While many newly built communities lack mature trees and vegetation, The Grow’s preservation space means that residents can live alongside nature from day one.

“It’s really about getting immersed in nature, and that’s really hard to find in new construction,” McNamara said. “Homebuilders typically buy a piece of land and clear it out, start from scratch, and then plant a lot of trees. But you’ve got another 30 years until you can really enjoy the shade from it. In this neighborhood, we’ve left hundreds of 100-year-old oaks all throughout the community. We’ve made them focal points.”

“One of the big draws is the lifestyle and the outdoor feeling that you’re going to have in this community,” he added

Claude AI Predicts and Backs Claver’s $10K XRP Price Prediction as Anthropic Faces Pompliano Backlash

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Brad Garlinghouse wants XRP to become the institutional gold standard for crypto rails, while David Schwartz sees blockchain transforming everyday finance. Now, Claude AI predicts XRP could reach $10,000, backing a bullish scenario popularized by Jake Claver as Anthony Pompliano takes aim at Anthropic’s new financial ambitions.

The prediction arrives as Ripple expands its institutional footprint, with Garlinghouse recently outlining a future where digital assets become central to global financial infrastructure. Schwartz has also envisioned blockchain becoming as accessible as the internet, powering payments, investments, lending, and everyday money management.

Meanwhile, Pompliano accused Anthropic of approaching engineers at his Silvia startup to obtain proprietary information before launching Claude Personal Finance. The controversy has put Claude’s financial ambitions in the spotlight, creating an interesting backdrop for its latest XRP price prediction.

Claude’s bullish case centers on XRP becoming the neutral bridge asset for global financial settlement. Banks, payment providers, and central banks could eventually use XRP to move value across borders instantly instead of maintaining pre-funded accounts across dozens of currencies.

That shift could create enormous demand for XRP as financial institutions build the liquidity needed to support international transactions.

Claude also highlighted XRP’s available supply as a potential catalyst. A significant portion of its supply remains locked in escrow, custody, and long-term wallets. As institutional adoption increases, the tradable float could shrink while demand continues growing.

The resulting supply-demand imbalance could trigger substantial price repricing.

Claude also identified regulatory clarity, tokenized assets, and stablecoins settling on the XRP Ledger as additional catalysts. Combined with growing demand for alternatives to traditional financial infrastructure, these developments could strengthen XRP’s position considerably.

Claver’s $10,000 prediction follows a similar thesis. He has argued that XRP’s role in global liquidity could eventually become vastly more valuable than its current market price suggests.

Garlinghouse’s latest comments add another interesting dimension. At Ripple Swell 2026, he described XRP as a potential institutional gold standard for crypto rails over the next five years. He also discussed moving more traditional financial activity onto blockchain infrastructure.

Caught up with @Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse and David Schwartz at Ripple #Swell to talk the future of @Ripple, institutional utility, and the path ahead for #XRP.Brad breaks down why 2026 has been a massive year for transaction volume growth, and David Schwartz shares his… pic.twitter.com/6zpWZGUcQG

Schwartz’s vision goes further, with blockchain eventually becoming part of how ordinary people manage money, receive payments, invest, lend, and borrow.

While XRP bulls anticipate a transformation in institutional payments, Bitcoin Hyper is targeting another enormous opportunity: bringing high-speed financial applications to the Bitcoin ecosystem.

Bitcoin remains the largest cryptocurrency by market cap, attracting an enormous audience of holders and investors. Bitcoin Hyper aims to tap into that liquidity through a Layer 2 network designed to introduce faster transactions and decentralized finance functionality.

Its architecture uses the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM), bringing Solana-style execution capabilities into a Bitcoin-focused ecosystem.

The ambition? It’s straightforward. Combine Bitcoin’s enormous liquidity and established investor base with transaction speeds designed for modern decentralized applications. Near-instant finality and low transaction costs are central to its pitch.

HYPER is also attracting attention through its presale pricing. The project has reportedly raised more than $33 million, showing considerable early fundraising momentum.

It offers 35% APY staking rewards, another incentive for early participants. Holders can stake their tokens and accumulate additional HYPER rather than simply leaving their allocation idle.

With the planned November 26 launch approaching, the presale is entering an important period. Those looking for exposure to Bitcoin’s expanding Layer 2 ecosystem are anticipating Hyper’s market debut before CEXes list it.

Claude’s $10,000 scenario ultimately comes down to XRP becoming a critical component of international financial infrastructure. Ripple already has a growing institutional network, with more than 85 licenses and partnerships spanning payments, custody, and digital asset infrastructure.

Its recent final approval for a national trust bank charter also strengthens its position within regulated financial markets. Meanwhile, RLUSD continues expanding Ripple’s stablecoin ecosystem, adding another layer to its institutional offering.

Ripple’s more than $16 trillion in transactions enabled across its products during 2025, and it expects to exit 2026 with a $900 million annualized product revenue run rate. These developments give Claver’s long-term thesis an increasingly ambitious backdrop.

If XRP captures a meaningful share of global settlement activity, institutional liquidity requirements could become a major driver of demand. Claude’s analysis highlights that the combination of expanding adoption and a shrinking tradable supply could create the conditions for an extraordinary repricing.

The $10,000 prediction is no longer just about another speculative price target. It is about how much value the global financial system could eventually place on a neutral digital settlement network.

TikTok Search Ads Are Becoming Real Search Advertising via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Match types, negatives, keyword bids, and impression share – here's what carries over from Google Ads for a paid search team and what doesn't.

In TikTok’s Oct. 30 Q4 product preview, two impression share metrics and a set of ready-to-use keyword packs were given to Search Ads advertisers. I wrote about this preview on Nov. 2. The quick version is that a help page defined those metrics back in September, and the packs are a new addition to the Keyword Suggestion Tool.

The long version is what a paid search manager sees when they open the campaign type for the very first time. A paid search manager has seen match type names, negative keyword lists and the search terms report before as part of Google Ads management. Match type reporting will depend on the campaign objective. Bidding by keyword needs the Traffic objective (along with a Cost Cap bid).

In each of these areas, for that manager, it becomes an issue of whether they can continue using some old habits they’ve developed, or if they need to change how they’re setting up their account and creating their reports. Each of these areas is addressed in TikTok’s help center.

Search advertising on TikTok has evolved sequentially during a four year period with the help center separating each addition. Brooke Osmundson reported that TikTok ran a search ads beta with select partner accounts in March 2022. The Search Ads Toggle was then made available to users in 2023. This is currently referred to as Automatic Search Placement, which extends in-feed ads into search result pages. All new campaigns will automatically be enabled with Automatic Search Placement. As noted by the help center at TikTok, Automatic Search Placement is considered “not a traditional keyword-based search ads offering.”

The keyword product Search Ads Campaign went live in the U.S. on Sept. 24, 2024. The two supported objectives were Traffic and Web Conversion. Also included with this launch were the keyword suggestion tool and negative keywords. At TikTok World in June 2025, TikTok stated “Billions of searches are happening on TikTok every day,” “up more than 40% from last year,” according to internal global data provided by the company, and did not provide an exact number.

On May 13, 2026, TikTok announced Search Hubs. This is a brand-owned page displayed above the search results for an approved list of keywords. TopSearch, which has a help center page dated October 2026, is a top-of-the-page auction for the first sponsored position and is billed per thousand impressions when using the Reach objective. When an advertiser loses in the auction, their ad “does not move into a lower search ad placement.” On Oct. 5, 2026, TikTok announced “Search Ads Campaign, powered by Smart+,” however there was no information regarding rollout dates in the post.

Automatic Search Placement, Search Ads Campaign, the Automated Keywords setting within Search Ads Campaign, Search Hubs and TopSearch are separate products and options; however the focus of this comparison is the Search Ads Campaign. The campaign page lists Traffic, Sales, Lead Generation and App Promotion as objectives. Additionally, the bottom of the campaign page states “available in the US and testing in other markets,” while TikTok’s availability page, dated April 2026, lists 11 countries for Traffic.

Keyword matching types on TikTok are called by the same name and have the same format as with Google Ads. On TikTok’s match type page it is explained that there are broad, phrase and exact. Phrase and exact are defined on the meaning of the search rather than its word order, so a phrase match for “Luxury fragrance” will also match a search for a specific perfume name. TikTok’s keywords page states you can have up to 1,000 keywords per ad group, but recommends you have at least 20.

Negative Keywords in both platforms have the same three match options, and TikTok allows a maximum of ten thousand negative keywords per ad group. As for plurals and synonyms, both platforms require the advertisers to include these variations. The platforms differ on misspellings. Google’s negative keywords automatically account for them. However, according to its documentation, TikTok’s negative keywords “don’t block singular or plural forms, misspellings, and synonyms” and “You must add each variation separately.”

TikTok’s search terms report is located where an individual managing a Google Ads campaign would look for it. In addition, TikTok’s reporting guide, dated April 2026, lists performance reporting at the search term, keyword and ad group levels. Traffic campaigns support keyword-level bids under Cost Cap. According to TikTok’s optimization guide, if a keyword has no assigned bid, the bid for that keyword will inherit the bid from its parent ad group.

TikTok’s Keyword Suggestion Tool generates “monthly impression predictions” it says “are based on historical data and are for reference only.” TikTok’s Keyword Planner provides “search volume, competition, and scale and performance prediction.”

The Oct. 30 preview of Keyword Packs includes pre-crafted lists of brand and category keywords. These lists generate “daily-refreshed recommendations” which include “key signals like search volume, CPC, and competition.”

The Impression Share help page, dated September 2026, has definitions for Impression Share as “The percentage of eligible search results impressions your ads captured” and Top 1 Impression Share as the same share of first-position impressions. The preview states Impression Share and Top 1 Impression Share are available at the campaign, advertiser and keyword levels. A health module in an ad group at the time of creation identifies thin keyword lists, low bids and a daily budget less than five times the bid.

Keyword-level bidding is documented for Traffic campaigns using Cost Cap, and the optimization guide says it “is based on the cost cap bidding, so it shows only when there is a valid bid.” The guide covers Web Conversion only through Target CPA but does not include keyword bidding. There is also an additional reporting restriction on match type keyword reporting, this one is Traffic only, and nothing on the pages reviewed as of Nov. 4 covers either control for Catalog or App Promotion campaigns. TikTok’s Automated Keywords feature, per a help page dated March 2026, is enabled by default for SMB accounts which “have to opt out or turn the toggle off,” while Key Accounts have the setting switched off by default.

TikTok’s page says “You aren’t notified when new automated keywords are added to a campaign.” Automated Keywords may be controlled with the toggle switch, by pausing an individual keyword, or through the use of negative keywords, and TikTok says its own internal testing showed the feature “can drive 10% incremental conversions while meeting CPA targets.”

All ads are either videos, carousels or, for Sales/Travel & Entertainment advertisers, a Search Catalog format. TikTok’s optimization guide says relevance is scored for each keyword and creative pair. Google measures ad quality as well; therefore, it is not the creative that differs between platforms. The production does differ because a campaign needs creative that fits each keyword theme.

Where the campaign type is sold varies based upon the objective of the campaign. TikTok’s availability page has two different country lists for Traffic. The first list includes Brazil, Australia, and all other countries’ advertisers who are trying to reach users in the U.S., and the second list includes Japan but not Brazil, Australia or the rest-of-world provision. Lead Generation campaigns are listed as available in the U.S. only. TopSearch campaigns are available to “Select accounts in supported markets,” and there are 20 countries listed for TopSearch.

Top 1 Impression Share is a related measure to Google’s Search absolute top impression share. The latter counts the number of impressions from “the very first ad among top ads” against the estimated number of impressions the advertiser would have been eligible for out of all the top ads. While both TikTok pages define the base of their metrics differently, “eligible search results impressions” according to the help page and “eligible search auctions” according to the preview, there are no publicly available resources showing the two platforms count the same way.

TikTok’s reporting guide says that keywords and search terms with low conversions “cannot be disclosed individually,” which “may cause a discrepancy in total conversions.” Keyword reporting is described as “a landscape report to provide insights on top-performing keywords” and full analysis, it says, should be done at the ad group and campaign levels, and not at the keyword level.

The keyword report provides you with the best-performing keywords, but may not reflect your campaigns’ total conversion numbers.

On the same reporting guide page it states that advertisers are unable to report on search terms or keywords broken out by creative (because of privacy). Data for iOS14 SKAN (Apple’s SKAdNetwork) for conversion, CPA, and conversion rate for Search Ads “are not currently available for the search ads campaign.” Brand safety and viewability measurement from third-party sources on the results page “may be subject to limitations.”

In addition, search campaign metrics lag behind by about eight hours. The guide says “Partial conversions will be available to advertisers after two days” and “Most conversions will display after three days.”

The automated terms report displays all automated terms as a single line item, with a drop-down menu showing the ten highest spending terms, the Automated Keywords page states. In addition, a search term that matches without an explicit keyword can display as “unknown.”

Neither the Impression Share help page nor the reporting guide indicates how to separate lost impression share by budget and by rank. On their help page, they list budget, bidding, keyword coverage, creative relevance, competition and changes in search demand as possible things to look at when assessing lost impression share; and sort the first two (budget and bidding) by if you spent your entire budget. Google’s page, by contrast, splits lost share into separate budget and rank metrics.

Reddit announced a test of ads in its search pages on Nov. 3 with a select group of eligible advertisers. The announcement says ads “may appear based on a range of factors, including contextual relevance to a user’s search query,” using Reddit’s existing targeting. It does not describe keyword controls.

In March 2023, Instagram announced ads in search results, showing “in the feed that people can scroll when they tap into a post from search results.” Both platforms announced a placement, while TikTok documented its keyword tools, a distinction for search teams to work with.

When you initially open the TikTok account, one of the first things you’ll recognize will be the search terms report. A manager who runs weekly Google search term reviews already has the expertise for that review, the negative keywords, and the ‘unknown’ rows.

The second item to verify when opening the TikTok account is whether the Automated Keywords toggle is turned on. The feature is enabled by default for SMB accounts, and additions arrive without notice.

Your campaign objective follows your business goal. According to TikTok’s optimization guide, if you run a Web Conversion campaign that cannot build volume, you can “temporarily switch to the Traffic objective to build volume,” and return to Web Conversion once the campaign is stable. During the Traffic phase, you can view match-type reporting and use keyword-level bids under a Cost Cap bid. Both of these disappear when you return to Web Conversion. However, keyword targeting (and its corresponding negatives), along with the search terms review, remain throughout.

Producing assets that match the keyword themes means working with whoever makes the company’s TikTok creative. Keyword totals show the top performers, TikTok points overall analysis to the ad group and campaign, and iOS14 SKAN conversion data is missing, so a cross-platform report needs a TikTok search row that says what it can and cannot show.

eMarketer’s U.S. search ad revenue forecast “excludes display-oriented formats on social networks, even if triggered by search queries,” and its social network ad spending forecast “includes all the various formats of advertising on those platforms.” That is how eMarketer draws its lines. This is useful context for anyone comparing TikTok search spend with its reported figures, though it doesn’t clarify where a company records the expense.

TikTok describes TopSearch as a visibility product. Its help center lists “brand, category, competitor, seasonal, or tentpole queries.”

As such, competitor queries listed as part of TikTok’s own lists constitute a brand-defense use case to assess, not a budget line to assume.

Paid search skills apply directly to TikTok’s keyword campaigns. The objective, automation defaults, creative requirements and reporting limits determine how far they carry. Budget ownership is still a company decision.

The campaigns for holiday promotions currently being run will be the ones described through the Impression Share columns in Ads Manager, on accounts where it shows them.

In addition, this quarter there will be a keyword-level read of spend on accounts that have the metrics. All accounts that have Automated Keywords enabled will see how those terms performed in campaign reporting and in the search terms report.

Reddit’s test is one to watch for anything more on search-specific keyword controls.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

Inside the Governor’s Mansions That Will Soon Be Home to 19 Newly Elected Leaders

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

In January, the United States’ 19 newly elected governors won’t just have inauguration day on their calendars, but moving day, too. All but five states have governor’s mansions for their leaders to live and work from, with the exceptions being Arizona, Idaho, Massachusetts, Rhode Island, and Vermont. These executive residences tend to be grand in style and scale, often possessing a long, rich history—even some haunted ones.

Sometimes, governors forego their official residences in favor of privately owned homes. For example, California governor Gavin Newsom recently listed the $7.5 million Fair Oaks home he lived in instead of the official executive residence, a 19th-century Victorian in Sacramento. Montana’s governor’s mansion is currently empty and in disrepair, while a governor hasn’t inhabited the official residence in Nebraska in over a decade.

Most states, however, have longstanding traditions of their leaders living in their official governor’s mansions. Virginia has the oldest continuously inhabited executive residence—every governor has lived there since 1813. If our new league of governors opts for tradition, here’s where they will be resting their heads for the next few years.

Republican Tommy Tuberville won Alabama’s gubernatorial race and will be able to move into a 119-year-old mansion in Montgomery following his inauguration. Built in 1907 by architect T. Weatherly Carter, the 17-room neoclassical residence features a row of four Corinthian columns at its front entrance, which opens to a foyer with a grand bifurcated staircase.

California’s new governor-elect is democrat Xavier Becerra, who will soon reside at a three-story Victorian in Sacramento. Built in 1877, the 20,000-square-foot house was thoroughly renovated in 2015, decades after Ronald and Nancy Reagan’s brief stint living there in 1967 before deeming it uninhabitable. The mansion was then used as a museum until its 2015 update, when it became home to the state’s governors once again. It has 30 rooms, Italian marble fireplaces, original wood floors, and a kidney-shaped swimming pool.

In Denver, democrat Phil Weiser will receive the keys to the red brick governor’s mansion known as Colorado’s Home. Built in 1908, it has been the state’s official governor’s residence since 1960. The Colonial Revival–style home has 27 rooms and shares its one-acre grounds with gardens, a historic gazebo, a greenhouse, and a carriage house. Thanks to John Hickenlooper, governor from 2011 to 2019, the home also has a rotating selection of local beers on tap.

Republican Byron Donalds just made history as Florida’s first Black governor. The state’s Greek Revival–style governor’s mansion in Tallahassee was completed in 1956 on the site of the original governor’s mansion built in 1907, which had fallen into disrepair. A screened swimming pool, a greenhouse, and a rose garden are among its amenities.

Democrat Keisha Lance Bottoms has the distinction of being the first Black woman governor-elect in the United States. Her soon-to-be residence in Atlanta is a 30-room Greek Revival mansion that was built in 1967 by architect A. Thomas Bradbury. Across three levels, it measures in at 30,000 square feet.

Iowa’s governor’s mansion, known as Terrace Hill, will soon be home to democrat Rob Sand. Dating all the way back to 1869, Terrace Hill is an 18,000-square-foot Second Empire–style residence in Des Moines where Iowa’s governors have lived since 1976. Its first two floors are open to the public as a museum and events space.

Democrat Hannah Pingree will move into the Blaine House in Augusta, Maine. Originally built in 1833 in a combination of Federal and Greek Revival styles, the mansion has been the state’s official governor’s residence since 1919, when renovations gave it a more Colonial style. It has 28 rooms across two stories, including a billiards room. The property also hosts a formal garden and a tennis court.

In Michigan, democrat Jocelyn Benson has the good fortune of governing a state with two official executive residences: one in the state’s capital of Lansing, and an official summer residence on Mackinac Island. The former is a 1957-built 8,700-square-foot ranch-style home with five bedrooms and four bathrooms; the latter is an Arts-and-Crafts-style mansion that was built in 1901.

Democrat Amy Klobuchar, currently a US senator for Minnesota, won her state’s gubernatorial election and will move into a 16,000-square-foot residence in St. Paul that dates to 1912. It features seven bedrooms, 10 bathrooms, and nine fireplaces. Architect William Channing Whitney designed the stone-and-brick dwelling in a combination of English Tudor and Beaux-Arts styles.

Nevada also elected its first Black governor, democrat Aaron Ford, who will soon reside in a Classical Revival–style mansion designed by architect George A. Ferris in 1909. It features a grand entrance hall, a reception room, a formal dining room, a governor’s study, and a luncheon room, in addition to private living quarters.

New Mexico’s governor-elect, democrat Deb Haaland, has made history as the first Native American woman to hold that title. In Santa Fe, the governor’s mansion is referred to as The People’s House. Designed by architect W.C. Kruger, the midcentury-modern structure was completed in 1955.

Democrat Amy Acton will be Ohio’s next governor, granting her residency inside a 1925 mansion in Bexley, just outside of Columbus. The Tudor-style home was built by architect Robert Gilmore Hanford, and its grounds boast three acres of extensive gardens, including a “Storybook Trail” inspired by The Tales of Peter Rabbit.

Republican governor-elect Mike Mazzei will be the new steward of Oklahoma’s official governor’s residence in Tulsa. The 14,000-square-foot Dutch Colonial was built in 1928 and has a limestone façade that matches the State Capitol building two blocks away. Uniquely, its grounds boast a swimming pool shaped like Oklahoma, an outdoor kitchen, and a tennis court on the former site of a helipad where President Lyndon B. Johnson landed in 1964.

Helena Foulkes, a democrat, won the race for Rhode Island’s governor, but she isn’t necessarily going to move out of her current residence any time soon, since the state doesn’t have an official governor’s mansion. Foulkes owns two homes in the state: a $3.8 million eight-bedroom summer house in Narragansett and a $2.3 million three-bedroom home in Providence.

Republican governor-elect Alan Wilson won South Carolina’s vote, making his next home a two-story Federal-style mansion dating back to 1855. Originally used as military academy officers’ quarters, it has been the official governor’s residence since 1868, three years after the Civil War ended. Inside, it has a central entrance hall connecting to two drawing rooms, a state dining room, and a library. Private living quarters are located on the second floor.

Marsha Blackburn, the Republican governor-elect of Tennessee, can move to a 10-acre estate in Nashville’s Oak Hill suburb. Known as Far Hills for the surrounding picturesque views, the three-story Georgian-style mansion was built in 1931. It has been the state’s official governor’s residence since 1949.

In Texas, democrat Gina Hinojosa will be the first Latina to hold the state’s highest office. Her official residence will be a Greek Revival–style mansion in Austin. Spanning nearly 10,000 square feet, it has been home to Texas’s governors since it was built in 1856.

Democrat David Crowley won in Wisconsin, making him the first Black governor of the state. The state’s executive residence is a Classical Revival–style mansion located in Madison. Completed in 1928, it has 16,000 square feet of interior living space across three floors, including seven bedrooms, 13 bathrooms, seven fireplaces, and 10 gardens.

Republican Eric Barlow will soon have access to the governor’s residence near Frontier Park in Cheyenne, Wyoming. In 1976, it replaced the state’s former executive residence, which was built in 1905 and is now open as a museum. Available details of the current mansion are sparse, however, and it seems that a new, upgraded version with improved security and functionality may soon be in the works.

The Best Ski Packs of 2027, Tested in the Backcountry

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Our 14 testers ran 18 packs through a season of fast transitions, steep bootpacks, and long days to find the best of 2027. (Photo: Rick Sorensen)

Overview: The best backcountry ski packs of 2027 include the Ortovox Venet 30 ($170) for most skiers, the Raide EX55 ($599) for expeditions, the Dynafit Radical 30 Snow Pack ($190), and the Rab Descendor 40 Ski Pack ($200) for ski mountaineering. Our 14 testers—men and women—evaluated 18 packs over a full season in Colorado, Utah, Alaska, the Alps, and more, judging fit, weight distribution, access, and durability.

When traveling in the backcountry, it’s not just what you bring but how you carry it. Intuitive access points make finding and stashing items easy, and externally fastened gear needs to stay secure without blocking pockets. But making the most of capacity is arguably the most critical feature. As our testers make tracks farther out in the mountains and become more self-reliant, they tend to reach for higher-volume packs that can fit everything they need without overloading them to the point of a skin track gear explosion. We factored comfort into all of it while testing 18 packs, narrowing the field to the best backcountry ski packs of 2027 for every type of ski adventure.

Pros and Cons + Triple-zipper access + Huge hipbelt pocket – A-frame carry blocks pack access and the diagonal carry strap was a little tricky to access

Ski packs work best when you can avoid taking them all the way off and rooting through the main compartment when you need to pull out gear. That means shorter stops in the skin track—if you stop at all—which helps you keep momentum and body temperature up on cold days. The Venet 30 features triple-zipper access to the main compartment, which let our tester quickly grab his water flask while skinning around Hemavan, Sweden, and Helgeland, Norway. The large hipbelt was also a tester favorite. “While I loved the elastic waist on the Ortovox Freerider, the normal hip strap with the pocket on the Venet was a huge plus for general touring,” said one. “On longer days I kept snacks in it and on more freeride days I kept a GoPro, for which easy access allowed me to actually remember to pull my cam out before descents.”

Testers called this the most ergonomic and versatile pack they’ve used from Ortovox, thanks to its simplicity, the comfortable adjustability of its straps, and its full-contact back panel. The mini top pocket was well sized—not too deep and easy to access—and the hidden mini pocket in the avalanche tool compartment was useful for carrying an emergency bivy and small med kit, keeping them “out of the way” of the rest of your gear.

This pack’s ideal use is general touring with the occasional bootpack, but it’s capable as a more technical bag when ropes may be required. It performs well as a freeride sidecountry bag or on longer tours—comfortable for mellow days and functional for technical ones.

Features that would make this pack even more user-friendly: a small zip on the right back side to mirror the bottom zipper for water access, the ability to carry two ice axes (the elastic cord holds only one), and easier access to the pack when using side ski-carry.

Pros and Cons + Modular volume + Rolltop closure – No external helmet carry

Schlepping all your gear on a big hut trip or expedition-style endeavor has long presented a dilemma: how to carry a pack big enough for everything at basecamp while also having a daypack small enough for lighter missions. Bringing two packs is inefficient, but skiing with a few items jiggling loose in a giant frame pack feels cumbersome. The Raide EX55 expands to 75L and carries enormous loads, then cinches down once you’ve emptied most items out, so it skis like a little daypack. “During our big four-day ski traverse out of Bishop in the Eastern Sierra, I’d get to the base of a couloir, fill a bivy sack with all our junk, cinch the pack down, and boot up like I had a regular ski pack on,” said our Mammoth-based tester. “It skis incredibly well given how much gear it can swallow.”

Raide accounts for the added weight with thicker shoulder and hip straps and better load lifters than its smaller counterparts, the LF30 and LF40. All of it is ideal for heavier hauls. The EX55 doesn’t just turn into a giant sack and skimp on key features. You can still deploy your probe without taking off your pack—ideal for glacier travel of any kind—and the same goes for the shovel with the pack on one shoulder. Testers called the ski carry systems excellent, and being able to grab an ice tool without removing the pack is invaluable in steep bootpacks. “This pack strips backcountry skiing down to its absolute essentials but lets you do it on a bigger scale,” said one.

The one complaint we heard was that the EX55 lacks the external drop sleeve found in the LF30 and LF40 for carrying helmets and skins, so when the pack is loaded for overnight carries, it’s tough to stuff skins in and out of the front zip pocket. It also makes the pack very tall when fully loaded with a helmet inside.

Weight: 2.31 lbs Size: Women’s, Universal

Pros and Cons + Thick back panel padding + Easy-access diagonal-carry bungee cord strap – Not ideal for those who pack extra gear

When you’re banging out hot laps, your pack should feel like part of your body. This one holds tight to smaller female torsos and disappeared on the back of another Sierra tester last spring as she skied 1,500- to 7,000-foot days on Mount Shasta, in the Eastern Sierra and at Lake Tahoe’s Rubicon Peak. “This pack skied really well, and gear that’s good enough to disappear is my favorite,” she said  after testing other packs with hipbelts that didn’t curve as ergonomically as this one.

Testers appreciated the top, rear-panel, and side zips, which made it easy to organize their stuff inside and keep things like crampons and water readily accessible. The avy gear gets its own pocket with a wide zipper, and an ice axe can be removed without taking the pack off, as can skis with a skimo-style diagonal carry bungee strap.

At 30 liters, it’s right on the edge of fitting everything you might want to carry on a day tour. “When I went out to the Norman Clyde Couloir, conditions were cold and windy, so I wanted to carry more than I usually do for a day tour,” said our tester. “The pack fit snacks, 1 liter of water, a hardshell, a puffy, ski and boot crampons, an ice axe, a helmet, a slender rescue sled, avy gear, a small first aid kit, and two pairs of gloves. I was definitely worried about busting the zipper, but it held all day.” The load lifters worked well to support this weight, and the thick back panel kept hard items like water bottles from poking into her back. If you prefer to bring this much stuff or more on most outings, you may find this pack a little too small.

Pros and Cons + Spot-on fit + Long side zipper accesses items of any size – Wobbly elastic diagonal ski carry

Spring ski mountaineering missions can mean longer days, longer approaches, and extra gear—crampons, more water, sometimes ropes—so you need a pack that carries it all in an efficient, functional arrangement. Loading it up is one thing; accessing it is another. The Descendor 40 balances both with a few key features. Our Colorado-based tester was a big fan of the side-access zipper, which is functionally useless if it isn’t long enough. This one is plenty long, and it was the only access point he needed while touring. He could easily pull out anything inside, including a large DSLR camera, skins, and a food bag, and he opened the rolltop closure only during full transitions.

The pack also has the best integration of hydration features. “On the right strap, there is a perfect pocket to carry a soft flask, including a strap to secure the flask against jostling,” our tester said. “I have lost soft flasks while skiing in other packs and am not worried about that on this pack. On the left shoulder strap, there is a small zippered pocket that is an appropriate size to carry items like gels, sunscreen, lip balm, etc. Overall, these features made it easy to stay hydrated and sunscreened on a fast and light ski tour.”

Testers lauded the comfortable back panel, wide hip straps, padded shoulder straps, and load lifters when needed. The pack carries weight well and fits true to size.

This pack is designed for long, efficient ski tours and glaciated summit pushes where ropes and technical gear are needed. It has features for ski mountaineering and carries additional gear like ice tools, with a gear loop for carabiners and a system for quickly strapping skis to the pack. The rolltop is an attempt to make the pack more versatile—a 40L pack that can act as a 25L—but it needs some refinement to better accommodate smaller volumes. Our tester also found the elastic strap for diagonal ski carry too long, and his skis bounced around while bootpacking couloirs.

Built for fast pow laps and and slackcountry days, the Dakine Heli 2.0 is everything you need to stay well equipped on your next mountain adventure. From diagonal ski and splitboard carry to vertical or horizontal snowboard carry, it doesn’t matter your style—it can get your gear wherever you need to go. The pack’s fleece-lined pockets keep your goggles smudge-free, and the snow tool sleeves keep your smaller items organized and accessible. And maybe most importantly, RECCO Technology helps keep you searchable in case of an avalanche or rescue.

Weight: 2.1 lbs (35L, S/M) Sizes: S/M, M/L

Pros and Cons + Easy access to inside storage + Removable snowboard carry straps – Avalanche tool pocket is too large

It’s a bold move to update a classic product, but Black Diamond took a leap on the tried-and-true Cirque by making several big changes. The brand added a side zipper to access the main compartment and more durable materials, including a PFC-free 210D nylon fabric and Dynex grid that’s stronger than the older version and more water repellent. Testers loved these new features and didn’t encounter any durability issues during the whole season of slamming the pack on and off in quick transitions with sharp ski edges and tools rubbing on the outside.

The new Cirque 35 excels on spring missions in the alpine, where quick access to crampons, an ice axe, and other gear is key. “I love a roll-top pack, and this is one feature that I really appreciate,” says one tester who toured it during winter and spring days around Aspen, Colorado. “I was able to throw in an extra layer for tours without hesitation because of the space it offered and how easy it was to get in and out, especially with the large side zipper.” The diagonal ski carry system with a bottom loop and top webbing strap with a hook was quick and easy to deploy in transition, and the ice-axe bungees were a breeze to unfasten on unexpected sections of steep terrain.

Our 6’1″ tester thought the pack was a bit short, and some disliked the hassle of opening both the webbing hook and roll-top buckle to get inside. Snowboard straps are sturdy but removable when you don’t need to carry a board on your pack.

Pros and Cons + Excellent freedom of movement + Compact fit – Limited carrying capacity

Skiing or snowboarding with too much extra space in a backpack allows the contents to rattle around inside, which can be an annoying distraction. Our Montana tester didn’t have that problem when hiking the ridge at Bridger Bowl, their local ski resort, with this compact, 15-liter pack with clever pocket placement and internal organization. Thanks to the low profile, it was comfortable enough to wear on the chairlift, and the fit felt snug while riding, too, with padding in all the right spots on the upper back, hips, and shoulder straps.

“It didn’t flop around at all like bigger packs do when empty, nor did it limit my movement in bumps or when really laying down carves,” another Montana tester said after securing the pack’s wide, sturdy hipbelt. They rode with this pack at Bridger Bowl in sub-freezing temperatures, requiring extra fuel and layers, and didn’t feel that it was too bulky. He also appreciated the glove-friendly A-frame and vertical ski carry options on short sidecountry hikes and bootpacks, along with snowboard straps and an internal avalanche tool compartment for sidecountry missions.

Overall, this is a small, space-efficient pack with smart pockets (fleece-lined goggle and external side zip) for short missions just beyond the gates or for fully-supplied resort laps.

Packs come in various sizes to fit different torso lengths, so measure your spine from the base of your neck to the top of your hips and choose your size accordingly. Torso lengths generally range from 15 inches, or size XS, to 20-plus inches, or size L. Because fit is the single most significant factor in pack comfort and carry, we recommend going to your local gear shop wearing the layers you plan to tour in and trying packs on. The hipbelt should securely wrap your iliac crest, and the back panel shouldn’t hit the back of your head when you’re wearing a helmet.

If half-day backcountry tours are your norm, choose a lower-volume pack, 20 to 30 liters, that fits just the essentials—shovel, probe, water, extra layers, snacks. If you typically spend full days in the backcountry, you’ll want a higher-volume pack, 30 to 40 liters, with room for the extra supplies you’ll need out in the elements. For overnight trips and expeditions, look for a 50-plus-liter pack.

Also make sure your avalanche shovel and probe slip easily in and out of the dedicated avy pouch in an emergency, and that the pack is big enough and organized enough for how you tour. Don’t be afraid to size up if you’re unsure how much space you’ll need—a few extra liters of empty space won’t weigh you down much. If you want to secure your skis to your pack, look for an A-frame or diagonal ski-carry strap system and back-panel suspension that can support the extra weight. The same goes for your helmet: most packs have integrated helmet carry, but not all do, so double-check if it’s essential to you.

Always carry avalanche safety equipment when traveling in the backcountry. That means wearing a beacon and carrying a shovel and probe in your pack. You’ll also need extra layers, a helmet, sufficient hydration—including something hot in an insulated bottle on longer backcountry days—snacks, a first-aid kit, and a basic repair kit.

To nail down the best ski packs of 2027, we seeded 18 products with 14 different testers. Each tester was matched to packs according to their planned objectives, body type, ability level, and testing environments. Then we set testers loose in the mountains, mainly in Colorado, Utah, Wyoming, Montana, Washington, California, and Alaska, with a few trips to Scandinavia and the Alps. Testers jammed gear into packs, stuffed them shut, and pushed them to their limits on both rugged and mellow ski ascents and descents.

We focused on newly designed packs with practical, ski-specific features that make traveling on snow easier, safer, and more enjoyable. We also brought tried-and-true, unchanged models into the mix so you’re seeing the best of what’s available. Comfort is a must, even for packs meant to carry smaller, lighter loads, as is durability, so we asked testers to evaluate straps, back panels, and hipbelt carry alongside the quality of materials.

Every time a pack went out, testers filled out feedback forms assessing fit, weight distribution, ease of access, and even pocket size and placement. Testers noted how easy it was to find what they needed, transition in the skin track, and carry their skis and boards on their backs while climbing steep couloirs. After a full season of rigorous testing, we selected the cream of the crop and reviewed those packs here.

Scott Yorko is a former gear editor for Backpacker, SKI, TransWorld SNOWboarding, and Outside. He lives in Colorado, where he splits his ski time between the world-class resorts around Aspen and the backcountry terrain of the West Elk Mountains. He loves to chase storms and visit pow-shredding friends, many of whom tested this year’s crop of ski packs in places like Alaska, Jackson Hole, the Sierra, Utah, and the Colorado Rockies.

Hideo Kojima Is 'Inventing A New Medium' - Bill Skarsgård Shares Why Starring In Xbox's PHYSINT Is A 'Big Deal'

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"I think Kojima is a pioneer, and keeps going out to untrodden waters"

Hideo Kojima always brings a certain sense of mystery and allure to his work, as is currently the case with the OD KNOCK and PHYSINT projects. For quite some time, speculation over who would star in the latter threw all sorts of crazy names into the mixer, before Kojima revealed that Pennywise and Dune actor Bill Skarsgård had got the job.

Skarsgård recently appeared on the Happy Sad Confused podcast, where he spoke about how working with Hideo Kojima is a "big deal" for him. He also admitted that he grew up playing all the Metal Gear Solid games and is a big fan of Kojima's process.

"I played every single one [Metal Gear Solid], at least the ones on platform. So, I was very enamoured by him and the sort of the mythology or the universes that he creates, but also the sort of mystique and allure about him as well."

As the actor continued to praise the Metal Gear creator, Skarsgård seemed to specifically be impressed by Kojima's fluent knowledge of filmmaking despite being a video game developer. He even recalled a moment where he asked Kojima if he would ever make a film:

"[Kojima] is an auteur, but he's a video game auteur. He's fluent in filmmaking, in film, and in cinema. I remember at one point, way back 20 years ago, there was talk about doing a Metal Gear Solid movie, and, is gonna Hideo gonna direct it? I asked him, 'would you ever do a film?' and he's like 'no'. At this point, what he's doing is inventing a new medium."

Skarsgård touched briefly on his performance capture in PHYSINT and suggested he's working on eight hours of narrative storytelling.

"What we're gonna do is eight hours worth of narrative, right? But it's also an entire universe that you play, so it truly is quite literally world building. But now with technology, we're getting closer and closer to something that's photorealistic, but it's also interactive when you play it. I think he's a pioneer, and keeps going out to untrodden waters."

PHYSINT is a return to tactical espionage for Kojima since departing Konami and Metal Gear Solid in 2015. We're very excited to see what he's been cooking up with Bill and the rest of the cast when we eventually learn more. Xbox has also remained fairly quiet on how PHYSINT fits into Microsoft's exclusivity plans.

Following BioWare Closure, a Documentary About the Legendary Studio Has Been Announced

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

In case you missed the news, publisher Electronic Arts recently pulled the plug on one of its most revered studios, BioWare, as part of a wider round of layoffs.

The developer, known for its many beloved contributions to the RPG genre, is sadly no more, and the game it was working on, a new Mass Effect, was also cancelled.

Interestingly, in the aftermath of this news, it's been announced that a documentary all about the studio is in the works, and will be released next year.

Titled Massive Effect: The BioWare Story, the film comes from Shane Fennessey and Adam Scorgie, producing for Canada‘s Super Channel.

It sounds like the documentary charts the company's history, from its founding in 1995 all the way through to its unfortunate closure just weeks ago.

According to a report from Deadline, filming for the documentary began two years ago, and is endorsed by BioWare founders Ray Muzyka and Greg Zeschuk.

They will appear in the doc, along with many other figures including Geoff Keighley, voice actor Jennifer Hale, former EA CEO John Riccitiello, and more.

Thomas Tuchel makes Noni Madueke decision as five Arsenal stars earn England call-ups

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Noni Madueke has missed another England opportunity, despite making an encouraging impression during his recent run in the Arsenal starting line-up.

Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain in November, but Arsenal winger Madueke misses out again after failing to make the cut following a missed international camp.

He has been left out despite making the most of Bukayo Saka’s absence to increase his involvement under Mikel Arteta.

Madueke scored in the recent Premier League win against Nottingham Forest and then again in Wednesday’s Champions League win against Slavia Prague.

He had a legitimate claim to be named in the squad, and Tuchel admitted that Madueke was very close to being included.

The England boss acknowledged that Madueke has benefitted from his extra games at Arsenal due to Saka’s injury.

But Tuchel chose to keep the same front three that he had during the last international camp. The German wants to reward those players who have earned their place in the team and not make changes every time they are playing well at club level.

This means Madueke will be left frustrated, having to wait until next time.

Madueke has played under Tuchel before, so it shows how much he thinks of him as a player. He featured in England’s 2026 World Cup campaign, so it is noticeable that he was left out this time.

Although he can’t control the battle for places, he will now be able to continue building his case. With Saka still injured, he will have the chance to play more regularly and solidify his place in Arteta’s plans.

However, Madueke is not the only player at Arsenal who has had positive news.

Declan Rice, Eberechi Eze, Ezri Konsa and Myles Lewis-Skelly have all earned or retained their spots in Tuchel’s squad. Ben White also comes back into the England squad, having missed the last camp.

It is another chance for White to re-establish himself on the world stage after his knee injury kept him out of the World Cup.

It also means Arsenal have five players in the squad after Saka was left out due to injury.

England play Croatia at Wembley on November 12 before heading to Spain on November 15. Madueke will be watching from home, knowing he was almost part of the squad.

Now he needs to build on his recent form and become a consistent performer for Arsenal. Whilst Tuchel has left the door open, Madueke needs to give the England boss another reason to change his mind.

Americans Bag Major First Ascent in Pakistan

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Americans Dane Steadman and Cody Winckler, awarded with a Piolet d’Or in 2025 after the first ascent of Yashkuk Sar, have done a similar monster 1,800m-long new route on the north face of Sosbun Brakk (6,414m). This time, Grant Stewart joined them for this off-season climb in the Karakoram.

This first ascent of the peak’s north face included difficulties up to M7, WI5. The climbers had to overcome several obstacles before even setting foot on the face. Like many other alpine-style teams this season, they were originally planning to climb another peak. But instead of adapting because of washed-out trails, they had bureaucratic issues. Their permit, obtained 10 months earlier, was revoked some weeks before their departure. They had to quickly find a different goal.

Meanwhile, the airline misplaced Winckler’s baggage — every adventurer’s nightmare. Somehow, the outfitter sent it to base camp some days later. Then, Steadman had to overcome serious Acute Mountain Sickness in base camp. Their acclimatization was hampered by freak spells of bad weather. Finally, the line on Sosbun Brakk they initially chose was too hard and risky because of a hanging serac they only spotted after they began.

The team’s lonely base camp. Photo: Dane Steadman

“Back at BC, it was time to rest, regroup, and contemplate a totally different line that we’d seen from on the wall, which gave us new hope,” Steadman recalled.

Off to the north face. Photo: Dane Steadman

On October 1, the three climbers set off on that potential new line. “Instead of angling left up hard mixed ground above the cone as before, we made an arcing traverse right, across an ice apron, to gain runnels on the right side of the face,” Steadman explained.

The team gained 800m on that first day and set their first bivouac on a snowy arete, just in time to be slammed by a violent storm that kept them in their tent the whole next day.

Steep mixed terrain. Photo: Dane Steadman

A tarp to protect the team’s bivouac from falling spindrift. Photo: Dane Steadman

“In the morning, we made a gamble for the top by leaving the tent and starting up a massive gully system,” Steward wrote. “At its top, we reached another dead end in a huge serac barrier. To escape this, Dane committed to a visionary pitch, casting off up a blank slab to eventually reach an ice smear.”

Chaotic, difficult rocky sections. Photo: Dane Steadman

“A huge serac to our right and smooth slabs out left posed a serious question; eight meters of sideways teetering on tiny gneissic edges and a miraculous rock thread were the answer,” said Steadman.

“We continued up the massive hanging glacier to reach the hallucinatory final headwall of steep ice/mixed climbing and mushroom-weaving lunacy,” Stewart continued. “One final traverse with 1,800m of exposure under our feet and a magically appearing, dead-vertical ice tongue led us to the summit ridge.”

A totally vertical section. Photo: Grant Stewart

“The wild terrain on the upper mountain took longer than we wanted,” Steadman admitted. By the time they finally reached the summit, it was 4 pm, the storm was coming the following day, and they still had a long way (and many rappels) down.

Summit picture on Sosbun Brakk. Left to right, Dane Steadman, Grant Stewart, and Cody Wrincker. Photo: Dane Steadman

“The sun would set around 6 pm, so we threaded the first of 35 rappels and started down. Long after dark, we finally arrived back at our bivy ledge at around 5,800m,” Steadman recalled.

The storm was not supposed to start until noon the following day, but it was already snowing at 8 am, with still 1,200 vertical meters of descent to go.

Rappelling down the route. Photo: Dane Steadman

“Before long, we were caught in a proper storm,” Stewart explained. “We committed to a faster and hopefully safer descent by rappelling down the west rib to the Sokha/Sosbun col. By the time we reached the col, we could barely communicate over the screaming southwest wind.”

After some scary rappels in high winds down the serac band, the climbers finally reached a sheltered section of the face. They still had to deal with heavy spindrift coming from above until finally, at around 4 pm, they touched down on the glacier. And yet, the adventure was not over — especially for Grant Stewart:

Following Dane, I took maybe 10 steps when I felt the ground under me crack. Instinctively, I jumped and landed on my stomach as a huge crevasse swallowed the ground behind me. Soaking wet, we began postholing into the wan light of a mesmerizing white out.

[We stuck] to the center of the glacier as avalanches rumbled down all around in the whirling snow. After a strange eternity, the ragged colors of a tent appeared and we were greeted by ecstatic dancing, big hugs, and endless cups of chai from (our BC crew) Ibrahim and Idrees.

Happy climbers and base camp crew after a safe return from the summit. Photo: Dane Steadman

The team didn’t report their success and the details of the climb until they returned home. And then, the first words of appraisal were not for the route itself, but for the mountains and people of Gilgit-Baltistan.

“It’s a unique place…where continents and cultures collide. Trips there, while often difficult, are rewarding in ways hard to describe simply,” Steadman said. This was his third trip to the Karakoram, a place he has fallen in love with.

Angela Benavides graduated university in journalism and specializes in high-altitude mountaineering and expedition news. She has been writing about climbing and mountaineering, adventure and outdoor sports for 20+ years.

Prior to that, Angela Benavides spent time at/worked at a number of local and international media. She is also experienced in outdoor-sport consultancy for sponsoring corporations, press manager and communication executive, and a published author.

Manchester United’s nine original ‘Fergie’s Fledglings’ & how they fared

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

While everyone remembers Manchester United‘s iconic Class of ’92, they weren’t the first group of young players that Sir Alex Ferguson worked with at Old Trafford.

Ferguson had some talented young players on his books in the late 1980s and they were the first group to be dubbed ‘Fergie’s Fledglings’ by the press.

We’ve taken a look back at Ferguson’s first batch of fledglings to see how they all fared.

After winning United’s Young Player of the Year award in 1987-88, Martin replaced Arthur Albiston and became their first-choice left-back over the following two seasons.

He only scored two goals for the club but one of them was the winner in the 1990 FA Cup final replay against Crystal Palace as Ferguson secured his first trophy at United.

But the defender struggled with injury problems after that FA Cup final win while Clayton Blackmore and Denis Irwin established themselves as United’s first-choice full-back pairing.

Martin was sold to Celtic in January 1994 and then had a spell at Bristol Rovers before seeing out his playing career in non-league football.

“If you’d told me at 16 I would play 110 games [for United] and score the winner in the FA Cup final I’d have snapped your hand off,” Martin told the Sun.

“I was a United fan, I still am, and it was just a great time. We all felt it was the start of a new team and to play my part in it is something I’m so proud of.”

Lee Martin scored in the #FACupFinal replay against Crystal Palace to secure Sir Alex Ferguson his first trophy as Manchester United manager. pic.twitter.com/dKaXvzuNX7

— Cantona Collars AKA Larry (@Cantona_Collars) May 17, 2021

Gill was handed his first-team debut early in Ferguson’s reign, filling in for the injured Bryan Robson during a 1-1 draw away against Southampton in January 1987.

“I’d never played in central midfield at United before so it was a surprise and I imagine very much a gamble for the manager,” Gill told Retro United.

“I was wearing Bryan Robson’s No.7 shirt and just so happened to wear New Balance boots at the time so I got a bit of stick in the dressing room along the lines of who did I think I was.”

But an Achilles tendon injury derailed Gill’s progress, keeping him on the sidelines throughout the 1987-98 season.

He started to become a first-team regular in 1988-89, only to suffer a broken leg and a shattered ankle during a game against Nottingham Forest in March 1989. Gill tried to recover but never played professional football again, retiring at the age of 22.

Ferguson wrote in 1992 that he would “undoubtedly have made it” if it wasn’t for injuries.

Alongside Martin and Gill, Wilson was part of the youth team that reached the 1986 FA Youth Cup final, but he made just six substitute appearances for United’s first team.

After loan spells at Lincoln City and Charlton Athletic, the midfielder was released by United at the end of the 1990-91 season.

He spent the next two seasons with Bristol Rovers before moving abroad, playing for teams in Finland and Sweden until his retirement in 2006.

Beardsmore made an immediate impact in United’s first team, registering a goal and two assists in a 3-1 win against Liverpool on just his second league start for the club.

He made a total of 73 appearances for the Red Devils but failed to live up to the initial hype and that Liverpool game ultimately proved to be the zenith of his United career.

The winger wasn’t named in the matchday squad for the 1990 FA Cup final or the 1991 European Cup Winners’ Cup final and failed to make a single Premier League appearance in 1992-93.

He joined Bournemouth on a free transfer in 1993 and made over 200 appearances during his five years at the club before a back injury forced him to retire at the age of 29.

Fergie's first fledglings of Lee Sharpe, Mark Robins and Russell Beardsmore combine to make it 3-1 against Liverpool on New Year's Day 1989

A lovely jinking run on the left by Sharpe and a wonderful volley from Russell Beardsmore past Mike Hooper #MUFC pic.twitter.com/miLt5BnSaD

— 80s&90sFootball ⚽ (@80s90sfootball) March 28, 2022

United fought off interest from Tottenham, Chelsea and Manchester City to sign a 17-year-old Brazil from Irish side Rivermount Boys in March 1986.

The right-back later became captain of United’s reserves but made just two cameo appearances for the first team before going on loan to Oldham Athletic and Swansea City.

“We were all looking over our shoulders at the Class of ’92 because we’d go and watch them play,” he told the 42. “They were only Under-16s, maybe, at the time but you’d sit there and watch and think, ‘Who are these boys?’ So we knew that was coming.

“And I think Fergie knew too and he almost used us as a template in many ways. A lot of us left because of the likes of those boys coming through.”

After spotting Gary Neville’s progress, he decided to join Cardiff City on a permanent deal in 1992 and went on to make over 100 appearances for the club.

Robins scored 17 goals in 70 first-team appearances for United, including the winner in an FA Cup third-round tie at Nottingham Forest which reportedly saved Ferguson’s job.

“I had no idea that goal against Nottingham Forest would become so important in the history of Manchester United,” Robins would later reflect in an interview with The Telegraph. “How could I at the time? Nobody knew it was a pivotal moment for Sir Alex Ferguson.”

But the striker failed to usurp Mark Hughes and Brian McClair and was deemed surplus to requirements following Dion Dublin’s arrival in the summer of 1992.

After joining Norwich City, he scored two goals in a 4-2 win against Arsenal on the opening day of the inaugural Premier League season and was also part of the side which famously defeated Bayern Munich in the UEFA Cup.

The 52-year-old left the Canaries in 1995 and then embarked on a nomadic career, playing for 11 different clubs over the following 10 years before hanging up his boots.

Despite being a prolific goalscorer in the youth teams, Graham made just four first-team appearances for United and scored his solitary goal for the club in an FA Cup tie against QPR.

The striker completed a £50,000 move to Barnsley in August 1991 but failed to make much of an impact at Oakwell, scoring just two goals in 42 appearances.

Short spells at Preston, Carlisle United, Stockport County, Scunthorpe and Halifax Town followed before he dropped into non-league football.

Unlike the Class of ’92, Fergie’s original fledglings didn’t just feature United academy graduates. Sharpe started his career at Torquay United before moving to Old Trafford shortly after his 17th birthday.

The winger quickly became a fan favourite at United due to his flair and won the PFA Young Player of the Year award in 1990-91 but was later usurped by the emerging Ryan Giggs,

While he didn’t live up to his potential, Sharpe still won three Premier League titles, two FA Cups, the League Cup and European Cup Winners’ Cup. He also won eight England caps, making him the only player on this list to have represented their national team at senior level.

Leeds United spent a club-record £4.5million on Sharpe in 1996, but he struggled with a series of injury problems and played just 35 times in three years, scoring six goals.

The 51-year-old later had stints at Bradford City, Portsmouth, Exeter City and Icelandic side Grindavik before retiring at Garforth Town.

Maiorana joined United from Eastern Counties League side Histon in a £30,000 deal after initially going on trial and impressing Ferguson in a friendly game against Birmingham City in 1988.

The winger showed glimpses of his raw talent during eight first-team appearances, but he had a turbulent relationship with the manager and was banished to the reserves.

“[Alex Ferguson] used to belittle me in front of people,” he told FourFourTwo. “Darren Ferguson would have his head in his hands, just shaking his head.

“Once, I went up to him and said, ‘What the f*ck has your dad got against me? What have I done to him?’ He didn’t know what to say. He was embarrassed. That happened on numerous occasions.”

After picking up a knee injury in a reserve game in 1991, he would only play five more first-team games in his entire career and they all came for Ljungskile SK in the Swedish second tier.
